 821: (d) After the `Resolve-FullSha` function, add:
 822: 
 823: ```powershell
 824: function Resolve-ReapPath($label, $raw, $repoTop, $commonFull, $headFull, $allowRemediation) {
 825:     # Returns the resolved full path of a tree this attestation may
 826:     # remove, or prints ERROR and exits 2. Every refusal here runs before
 827:     # the record is written. The rules are the spec's identity guard:
 828:     # rooted and resolvable, not a filesystem root, an existing
 829:     # directory, not through a link, not overlapping the reviewed repo
 830:     # or its common dir, a .git DIRECTORY (a .git FILE is a linked
 831:     # worktree, never a mirror or a bridge), and a HEAD equal to the
 832:     # attested head - or, for the mirror only, one parallax@local
 833:     # remediation commit whose single parent is that head, which is the
 834:     # commit the mirror tool makes over a tracked back-channel.
 835:     if ([string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($raw)) {
 836:         Write-Output "ERROR: $label is empty"
 837:         exit 2
 838:     }
 839:     if (-not [System.IO.Path]::IsPathRooted($raw)) {
 840:         Write-Output "ERROR: $label must be an absolute path ($raw)"
 841:         exit 2
 842:     }
 843:     $full = $null
 844:     try {
 845:         $full = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($raw).TrimEnd("\")
 846:     } catch {
 847:         Write-Output ("ERROR: $label could not be resolved ($raw): " + $_.Exception.Message)
 848:         exit 2
 849:     }
 850:     $pathRoot = [System.IO.Path]::GetPathRoot($full)
 851:     if ((-not $pathRoot) -or ($full.Length -le ([string]$pathRoot).TrimEnd("\").Length)) {
 852:         Write-Output "ERROR: $label is a filesystem root ($full)"
 853:         exit 2
 854:     }
 855:     $attr = 0
 856:     try {
 857:         $attr = [int][System.IO.File]::GetAttributes($full)
 858:     } catch [System.IO.FileNotFoundException] {
 859:         Write-Output "ERROR: $label does not exist ($full)"
 860:         exit 2
 861:     } catch [System.IO.DirectoryNotFoundException] {
 862:         Write-Output "ERROR: $label does not exist ($full)"
 863:         exit 2
 864:     } catch {
 865:         Write-Output ("ERROR: $label could not be examined ($full): " + $_.Exception.Message)
 866:         exit 2
 867:     }
 868:     if (($attr -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::Directory) -eq 0) {
 869:         Write-Output "ERROR: $label is a file, not a tree ($full)"
 870:         exit 2
 871:     }
 872:     $hit = Test-PathOrAncestorIsLink $full
 873:     if ($hit) {
 874:         Write-Output ("ERROR: $label passes through a directory link at $hit" +
 875:             " - a tree is never removed through a link")
 876:         exit 2
 877:     }
 878:     # The mirror tool's own overlap comparison, against the reviewed
 879:     # repository and against its git common dir (a linked worktree's
 880:     # common dir sits outside its top level).
 881:     $cmp = [System.StringComparison]::OrdinalIgnoreCase
 882:     $p = $full.Replace("\", "/").TrimEnd("/") + "/"
 883:     foreach ($pair in @(@("the reviewed repository", $repoTop), @("the git common dir", $commonFull))) {
 884:         $g = ([string]$pair[1]).Replace("\", "/").TrimEnd("/") + "/"
 885:         if ($p.Equals($g, $cmp)) {
 886:             Write-Output ("ERROR: $label is " + $pair[0] + " itself ($full)")
 887:             exit 2
 888:         }
 889:         if ($p.StartsWith($g, $cmp)) {
 890:             Write-Output ("ERROR: $label is inside " + $pair[0] + " ($full)")
 891:             exit 2
 892:         }
 893:         if ($g.StartsWith($p, $cmp)) {
 894:             Write-Output ("ERROR: $label contains " + $pair[0] + " ($full)")
 895:             exit 2
 896:         }
 897:     }
 898:     $dotGit = Join-Path $full ".git"
 899:     $ga = 0
 900:     try {
 901:         $ga = [int][System.IO.File]::GetAttributes($dotGit)
 902:     } catch {
 903:         Write-Output "ERROR: $label carries no .git entry, so it is not a review tree ($full)"
 904:         exit 2
 905:     }
 906:     if (($ga -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::Directory) -eq 0) {
 907:         Write-Output ("ERROR: $label has a .git FILE, which marks a linked worktree" +
 908:             " - never a mirror or a bridge ($full)")
 909:         exit 2
 910:     }
 911:     $treeHead = (& git -C $full rev-parse --verify --quiet "HEAD^{commit}" 2>$null | Out-String).Trim()
 912:     if (($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) -or -not $treeHead) {
 913:         Write-Output "ERROR: $label has no readable HEAD ($full)"
 914:         exit 2
 915:     }
 916:     if ($treeHead -eq $headFull) {
 917:         return $full
 918:     }
 919:     if ($allowRemediation) {
 920:         $author = (& git -C $full log -1 --format=%ae HEAD 2>$null | Out-String).Trim()
 921:         $authorExit = $LASTEXITCODE
 922:         $parents = @(((& git -C $full rev-list --parents -n 1 HEAD 2>$null | Out-String).Trim()) -split "\s+")
 923:         if (($authorExit -eq 0) -and ($LASTEXITCODE -eq 0) -and ($author -eq "parallax@local") -and
 924:             ($parents.Count -eq 2) -and ($parents[1] -eq $headFull)) {
 925:             return $full
 926:         }
 927:     }
 928:     Write-Output ("ERROR: $label is at $treeHead, not the attested head $headFull" +
 929:         " - it is not the tree this verdict was issued on ($full)")
 930:     exit 2
 931: }
 932: 
 933: function Invoke-Reap($label, $full) {
 934:     # Runs after the record is written: a failure here is exit 3, with
 935:     # the attestation left standing and the reason named.
 936:     $r = Remove-ReviewTree $full
 937:     if (-not $r.Ok) {
 938:         Write-Output ("ERROR: reap failed for " + $full + ": " + $r.Reason +
 939:             " - the attestation stands; remove the " + $label + " by hand")
 940:         exit 3
 941:     }
 942:     Write-Output ("reaped " + $label + ": " + $full)
 943: }
 944: ```
 945: 
 946: (e) Immediately BEFORE the line `$attDir = Join-Path (Join-Path $commonDir "parallax") "attestations"`, add:
 947: 
 948: ```powershell
 949: # REAP VALIDATION, before anything is written. Both trees are resolved
 950: # and checked here so a wrong argument is refused at exit 2 with the
 951: # record unwritten, and the removal below runs only against paths this
 952: # block accepted.
 953: $commonFull = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($commonDir).TrimEnd("\")
 954: $reapMirrorFull = $null
 955: $reapBridgeFull = $null
 956: if ($ReapMirror) {
 957:     $reapMirrorFull = Resolve-ReapPath "the reap mirror" $ReapMirror $toplevel $commonFull $headFull $true
 958: }
 959: if ($ReapBridge) {
 960:     $reapBridgeFull = Resolve-ReapPath "the reap bridge" $ReapBridge $toplevel $commonFull $headFull $false
 961: }
 962: if ($reapMirrorFull -and $reapBridgeFull -and ($reapMirrorFull -ieq $reapBridgeFull)) {
 963:     Write-Output "ERROR: the reap mirror and the reap bridge are the same tree ($reapMirrorFull)"
 964:     exit 2
 965: }
 966: ```

 969: 
 970: ```powershell

 972: exit 0

 974: 
 975: with:
 976: 
 977: ```powershell
 978: Write-Output "attestation written: $outFile ($Verdict, $baseFull..$headFull)"
 979: # THE REAP, after the record and in this order: mirror, its advisory
 980: # sidecar, then the bridge. A failure stops at the first tree that
 981: # could not be removed (exit 3) and leaves the record standing.
 982: if ($reapMirrorFull) {
 983:     Invoke-Reap "mirror" $reapMirrorFull
 984:     # The mirror tool's advisory sibling, `<mirror>.source-manifest`,
 985:     # removed only when it is an ordinary file: a directory or a link
 986:     # there is not the sidecar and is left alone.
 987:     $sidecar = $reapMirrorFull + ".source-manifest"
 988:     $sa = $null
 989:     try {
 990:         $sa = [int][System.IO.File]::GetAttributes($sidecar)
 991:     } catch {
 992:         $sa = $null
 993:     }
 994:     if (($null -ne $sa) -and
 995:         (($sa -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::Directory) -eq 0) -and
 996:         (($sa -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::ReparsePoint) -eq 0)) {
 997:         try {
 998:             [System.IO.File]::SetAttributes($sidecar, [System.IO.FileAttributes]::Normal)
 999:             [System.IO.File]::Delete($sidecar)
1000:         } catch {
1001:             Write-Output ("ERROR: reap failed for " + $sidecar + ": " +
1002:                 $_.Exception.Message + " - the attestation stands; remove the sidecar by hand")
1003:             exit 3
1004:         }
1005:         Write-Output ("reaped sidecar: " + $sidecar)
1006:     }
1007: }
1008: if ($reapBridgeFull) {
1009:     Invoke-Reap "bridge" $reapBridgeFull
1010: }
1011: exit 0
1012: ```

1044: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------

1128: - [ ] **Step 4: Append the end-of-life section to preflight-mirror.md**
1129: 
1130: Append to the END of `skills/multi-model-verify/references/preflight-mirror.md`:
1131: 
1132: ```markdown
1133: 
1134: ## End of life
1135: 
1136: The attestation is the reap point. A round's `resume` re-verifies the
1137: mirror's identity, so a mirror deleted mid-debate turns a resumable
1138: round into a transport failure; the one terminal event the plugin
1139: records mechanically is the attestation, so `tools/write-attestation.ps1`
1140: removes the mirror when it is passed as `-ReapMirror <path>`, and the
1141: clone bridge a linked worktree needed when it is passed as
1142: `-ReapBridge <path>`. The reap is bound to that event and never an age:
1143: no sweep exists, and the doctor only reports.
1144: 
1145: The emitter validates both paths BEFORE it writes the record, so a wrong
1146: argument is refused at exit 2 with nothing written, and removes them
1147: AFTER, so a removal that fails leaves the verdict standing and exits 3
1148: naming the entry that stopped it. A path is accepted only when it
1149: exists as a directory not reached through a link, does not overlap the
1150: reviewed repository or its git common dir, holds a `.git` DIRECTORY
1151: (a `.git` FILE marks a linked worktree, never a mirror or a bridge), and
1152: its HEAD is the attested head. The mirror may instead sit exactly one
1153: `parallax@local` remediation commit above that head, because that is the
1154: commit construction makes over a tracked back-channel; the bridge must
1155: match exactly, so a bridge left unfetched after a fix commit is refused
1156: rather than deleted under a stale head. Measured 2026-09-13: 78 mirror
1157: and bridge directories, 13.4 GB, in four review days, with nothing but
1158: memory saying which of them a live chat could still resume. Another
1159: chat's mirror is at another head and is refused by name.
1160: 
1161: The removal never recurses through a link: `tools/review-tree-removal.ps1`
1162: walks the tree itself, removes each link as a link, clears the read-only
1163: bit git puts on its objects, and re-examines the root afterwards. The
1164: mirror tool's `-Force` rebuild uses the same function, which is what
1165: closed backlog item 98.
1166: 
1167: An existing `-MirrorPath` without `-Force` is refused with the reap
1168: route named. Build `kv-<tag>-2` beside a finished debate's mirror and
1169: the count grows by one for every debate; reap the finished one instead,
1170: and rebuild in place with `-Force` only for a debate that is still
1171: running, because a resumed round needs the mirror at the path its
1172: identity was recorded at.
1173: 
1174: The bridge is the session's. The plugin never created it and cannot
1175: recognise one by shape, so the session that built it names it; the rule
1176: "pass the bridge to the emitter" belongs beside the rule that builds it.
1177: ```

1185: ## 10. Review mirror inventory
1186: 
1187: Observation only: this check reports and never deletes, whatever it
1188: finds. List every DIRECTORY whose name begins with `kv` sitting directly
1189: under `$env:SystemDrive\` and directly under `$env:TEMP` - the two
1190: places a review mirror or its clone bridge is built (the canonical
1191: review mirror root is the temp directory; a session whose packets blow
1192: the path budget builds at the drive root instead, and both are outside
1193: every checkout). Measure the count, the total size in GB (sum of file
1194: lengths, reparse points NOT followed), and the oldest `LastWriteTime`
1195: among them. A directory that cannot be measured is named as unmeasured
1196: and counted; it never reads as empty.
1197: 
1198: - Nothing found: OK, `no review mirrors present`.
1199: - Found, total under 5 GB AND oldest under 3 days: OK, reported as a
1200:   NOTE with the three numbers.
1201: - Total 5 GB or more, OR oldest 3 days or more: STALE, with the three
1202:   numbers and this fix: a finished debate's mirror is removed by the
1203:   attestation emitter, `write-attestation.ps1 -ReapMirror <mirror>
1204:   [-ReapBridge <bridge>]`, and one whose debate is over without an
1205:   attestation is removed by hand; the rule and its measurement are
1206:   backlog item 101. Never name a directory as safe to delete: the
1207:   doctor cannot tell which of them a live chat can still resume, and a
1208:   `resume` against a deleted mirror is a transport failure.

1211: tripped on day two: 13.4 GB across 78 directories accumulated in four
1212: review days, about 3 GB per active day.

   1: # write-attestation.ps1 - record a multi-model-verify diff-mode TERMINAL
   2: # verdict as a machine-readable, SHA-bound attestation.
   3: #
   4: # Written by the SESSION after its final adjudication - never from the
   5: # reviewer's reply alone; the session's terminal verdict is the thing being
   6: # recorded (family standing rule: a reviewer PASS/FIX is input, not
   7: # terminal). Stored under the repo's GIT COMMON DIR, not the working tree:
   8: # writing it cannot move HEAD out from under its own SHA, it never ships in
   9: # a commit, and worktrees share it. verify-attestation.ps1 is the consumer
  10: # (pre-push lanes, non-blocking v1).
  11: #
  12: # Windows PowerShell 5.1 compatible, ASCII ONLY (see check-drift.ps1
  13: # header for why).
  14: #
  15: # Exit codes: 0 written, 2 argument/repo error, 3 written but a reap failed.
  16: #
  17: # REAP (0.35.0, backlog item 101): -ReapMirror and -ReapBridge name the
  18: # review mirror and the clone bridge the debate ran on. The attestation
  19: # is the one TERMINAL event the plugin records mechanically, so it is
  20: # the reap point - never an age. Both paths are validated against the
  21: # reviewed repository and the attested head BEFORE the record is
  22: # written, so a refused argument costs nothing (exit 2); they are
  23: # removed AFTER it, so a removal failure leaves the verdict standing
  24: # and says so (exit 3). The removal itself is
  25: # tools/review-tree-removal.ps1, shared with the mirror tool.
  26: param(
  27:     [Parameter(Mandatory = $true)][string]$RepoRoot,
  28:     [Parameter(Mandatory = $true)][string]$BaseSha,
  29:     [Parameter(Mandatory = $true)][string]$HeadSha,
  30:     [Parameter(Mandatory = $true)][ValidateSet("PASS", "FIX", "ESCALATE")][string]$Verdict,
  31:     [Parameter(Mandatory = $true)][int]$Rounds,
  32:     [Parameter(Mandatory = $true)][string]$Participants,
  33:     # Both mandatory, no defaults (Sol diff review 0.6.0): a defaulted
  34:     # success value would let an emitter that never checked the route or
  35:     # the verification status mint a gate-satisfying record.
  36:     [Parameter(Mandatory = $true)][ValidateSet("FULL", "DEGRADED")][string]$VerificationStatus,
  37:     [Parameter(Mandatory = $true)][string]$RouteNote,
  38:     [string]$Mode = "diff",
  39:     # Optional (0.7.0): the application checkpoint that authorized the fix
  40:     # edits inside the attested range (references/application-checkpoint.md).
  41:     # Must live under the checkpoint row of
  42:     # references/model-prompting-notes.md's round-artifact-roots
  43:     # declaration - the verifier re-locates and re-hashes it there, so
  44:     # an artifact anywhere else is unverifiable.
  45:     # When present, the record binds the checkpoint hash AND the
  46:     # emitter-computed changed-path set - never caller-supplied - so an
  47:     # attestation minted for a different change set fails verification.
  48:     [string]$CheckpointFile = "",
  49:     # Optional (0.35.0): the review mirror and the clone bridge to remove
  50:     # once the record is written. See the REAP note in the header.
  51:     [string]$ReapMirror = "",
  52:     [string]$ReapBridge = ""
  53: )
  54: 
  55: . (Join-Path $PSScriptRoot "review-tree-removal.ps1")
  56: 
  57: function Resolve-FullSha($repo, $sha, $label) {
  58:     $full = (& git -C $repo rev-parse --verify --quiet ($sha + "^{commit}") 2>$null | Out-String).Trim()
  59:     if (($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) -or -not $full) {
  60:         Write-Output "ERROR: $label '$sha' does not resolve to a commit in $repo"
  61:         exit 2
  62:     }
  63:     return $full
  64: }
  65: 
  66: function Resolve-ReapPath($label, $raw, $repoTop, $commonFull, $headFull, $allowRemediation) {
  67:     # Returns the resolved full path of a tree this attestation may
  68:     # remove, or prints ERROR and exits 2. Every refusal here runs before
  69:     # the record is written. The rules are the spec's identity guard:
  70:     # rooted and resolvable, not a filesystem root, an existing
  71:     # directory, not through a link, not overlapping the reviewed repo
  72:     # or its common dir, a .git DIRECTORY (a .git FILE is a linked
  73:     # worktree, never a mirror or a bridge), and a HEAD equal to the
  74:     # attested head - or, for the mirror only, one parallax@local
  75:     # remediation commit whose single parent is that head, which is the
  76:     # commit the mirror tool makes over a tracked back-channel.
  77:     if ([string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($raw)) {
  78:         Write-Output "ERROR: $label is empty"
  79:         exit 2
  80:     }
  81:     if (-not [System.IO.Path]::IsPathRooted($raw)) {
  82:         Write-Output "ERROR: $label must be an absolute path ($raw)"
  83:         exit 2
  84:     }
  85:     $full = $null
  86:     try {
  87:         $full = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($raw).TrimEnd("\")
  88:     } catch {
  89:         Write-Output ("ERROR: $label could not be resolved ($raw): " + $_.Exception.Message)
  90:         exit 2
  91:     }
  92:     $pathRoot = [System.IO.Path]::GetPathRoot($full)
  93:     if ((-not $pathRoot) -or ($full.Length -le ([string]$pathRoot).TrimEnd("\").Length)) {
  94:         Write-Output "ERROR: $label is a filesystem root ($full)"
  95:         exit 2
  96:     }
  97:     $attr = 0
  98:     try {
  99:         $attr = [int][System.IO.File]::GetAttributes($full)
 100:     } catch [System.IO.FileNotFoundException] {
 101:         Write-Output "ERROR: $label does not exist ($full)"
 102:         exit 2
 103:     } catch [System.IO.DirectoryNotFoundException] {
 104:         Write-Output "ERROR: $label does not exist ($full)"
 105:         exit 2
 106:     } catch {
 107:         Write-Output ("ERROR: $label could not be examined ($full): " + $_.Exception.Message)
 108:         exit 2
 109:     }
 110:     if (($attr -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::Directory) -eq 0) {
 111:         Write-Output "ERROR: $label is a file, not a tree ($full)"
 112:         exit 2
 113:     }
 114:     $hit = Test-PathOrAncestorIsLink $full
 115:     if ($hit) {
 116:         Write-Output ("ERROR: $label passes through a directory link at $hit" +
 117:             " - a tree is never removed through a link")
 118:         exit 2
 119:     }
 120:     # The mirror tool's own overlap comparison, against the reviewed
 121:     # repository and against its git common dir (a linked worktree's
 122:     # common dir sits outside its top level).
 123:     $cmp = [System.StringComparison]::OrdinalIgnoreCase
 124:     $p = $full.Replace("\", "/").TrimEnd("/") + "/"
 125:     foreach ($pair in @(@("the reviewed repository", $repoTop), @("the git common dir", $commonFull))) {
 126:         $g = ([string]$pair[1]).Replace("\", "/").TrimEnd("/") + "/"
 127:         if ($p.Equals($g, $cmp)) {
 128:             Write-Output ("ERROR: $label is " + $pair[0] + " itself ($full)")
 129:             exit 2
 130:         }
 131:         if ($p.StartsWith($g, $cmp)) {
 132:             Write-Output ("ERROR: $label is inside " + $pair[0] + " ($full)")
 133:             exit 2
 134:         }
 135:         if ($g.StartsWith($p, $cmp)) {
 136:             Write-Output ("ERROR: $label contains " + $pair[0] + " ($full)")
 137:             exit 2
 138:         }
 139:     }
 140:     $dotGit = Join-Path $full ".git"
 141:     $ga = 0
 142:     try {
 143:         $ga = [int][System.IO.File]::GetAttributes($dotGit)
 144:     } catch {
 145:         Write-Output "ERROR: $label carries no .git entry, so it is not a review tree ($full)"
 146:         exit 2
 147:     }
 148:     if (($ga -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::Directory) -eq 0) {
 149:         Write-Output ("ERROR: $label has a .git FILE, which marks a linked worktree" +
 150:             " - never a mirror or a bridge ($full)")
 151:         exit 2
 152:     }
 153:     if (($ga -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::ReparsePoint) -ne 0) {
 154:         Write-Output ("ERROR: $label has a .git that is a directory link, so its" +
 155:             " identity would be read through the link ($full)")
 156:         exit 2
 157:     }
 158:     $treeHead = (& git --git-dir "$dotGit" rev-parse --verify --quiet "HEAD^{commit}" 2>$null | Out-String).Trim()
 159:     if (($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) -or -not $treeHead) {
 160:         Write-Output "ERROR: $label has no readable HEAD ($full)"
 161:         exit 2
 162:     }
 163:     if ($treeHead -eq $headFull) {
 164:         return $full
 165:     }
 166:     if ($allowRemediation) {
 167:         $author = (& git --git-dir "$dotGit" log -1 --format=%ae HEAD 2>$null | Out-String).Trim()
 168:         $authorExit = $LASTEXITCODE
 169:         $parents = @(((& git --git-dir "$dotGit" rev-list --parents -n 1 HEAD 2>$null | Out-String).Trim()) -split "\s+")
 170:         if (($authorExit -eq 0) -and ($LASTEXITCODE -eq 0) -and ($author -eq "parallax@local") -and
 171:             ($parents.Count -eq 2) -and ($parents[1] -eq $headFull)) {
 172:             return $full
 173:         }
 174:     }
 175:     Write-Output ("ERROR: $label is at $treeHead, not the attested head $headFull" +
 176:         " - it is not the tree this verdict was issued on ($full)")
 177:     exit 2
 178: }
 179: 
 180: function Invoke-Reap($label, $full, $notAttempted) {
 181:     # Runs after the record is written: a failure here is exit 3, with
 182:     # the attestation left standing and the reason named. $notAttempted
 183:     # names any other reap tree that was skipped as a result, so the
 184:     # failure message tells the caller everything left to remove by hand.
 185:     $r = Remove-ReviewTree $full
 186:     if (-not $r.Ok) {
 187:         Write-Output ("ERROR: reap failed for " + $full + ": " + $r.Reason +
 188:             " - the attestation stands; remove the " + $label + " by hand" + $notAttempted)
 189:         exit 3
 190:     }
 191:     Write-Output ("reaped " + $label + ": " + $full)
 192: }
 193: 
 194: $toplevel = (& git -C $RepoRoot rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>$null | Out-String).Trim()
 195: if (($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) -or -not $toplevel) {
 196:     Write-Output "ERROR: $RepoRoot is not a git repository"
 197:     exit 2
 198: }
 199: # Common dir, not git-dir: a review adjudicated inside a worktree must
 200: # attest the repo every other worktree (and the pre-push hook) sees.
 201: $commonDir = (& git -C $RepoRoot rev-parse --git-common-dir 2>$null | Out-String).Trim()
 202: if (-not $commonDir) {
 203:     Write-Output "ERROR: could not resolve the git common dir for $RepoRoot"
 204:     exit 2
 205: }
 206: if (-not [System.IO.Path]::IsPathRooted($commonDir)) {
 207:     $commonDir = Join-Path $RepoRoot $commonDir
 208: }
 209: 
 210: $baseFull = Resolve-FullSha $RepoRoot $BaseSha "BaseSha"
 211: $headFull = Resolve-FullSha $RepoRoot $HeadSha "HeadSha"
 212: if ($baseFull -eq $headFull) {
 213:     Write-Output "ERROR: BaseSha and HeadSha resolve to the same commit - nothing was reviewed"
 214:     exit 2
 215: }
 216: 
 217: # REAP VALIDATION, before anything is written. Both trees are resolved
 218: # and checked here so a wrong argument is refused at exit 2 with the
 219: # record unwritten, and the removal below runs only against paths this
 220: # block accepted.
 221: $commonFull = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($commonDir).TrimEnd("\")
 222: $reapMirrorFull = $null
 223: $reapBridgeFull = $null
 224: if ($ReapMirror) {
 225:     $reapMirrorFull = Resolve-ReapPath "the reap mirror" $ReapMirror $toplevel $commonFull $headFull $true
 226: }
 227: if ($ReapBridge) {
 228:     $reapBridgeFull = Resolve-ReapPath "the reap bridge" $ReapBridge $toplevel $commonFull $headFull $false
 229: }
 230: if ($reapMirrorFull -and $reapBridgeFull) {
 231:     $cmp = [System.StringComparison]::OrdinalIgnoreCase
 232:     $m = $reapMirrorFull.Replace("\", "/").TrimEnd("/") + "/"
 233:     $b = $reapBridgeFull.Replace("\", "/").TrimEnd("/") + "/"
 234:     if ($m.Equals($b, $cmp) -or $m.StartsWith($b, $cmp) -or $b.StartsWith($m, $cmp)) {
 235:         Write-Output ("ERROR: the reap mirror and the reap bridge overlap (" + $reapMirrorFull + ", " + $reapBridgeFull + ") - name two separate trees")
 236:         exit 2
 237:     }
 238: }
 239: 
 240: $attDir = Join-Path (Join-Path $commonDir "parallax") "attestations"
 241: New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $attDir | Out-Null
 242: 
 243: # Schema 2 (0.7.0): checkpoint_binding is the emitter-authored
 244: # DECLARATION of whether this record carries checkpoint metadata -
 245: # without it, deleting the binding fields would downgrade a bound record
 246: # to legacy-unbound and the verifier could not tell (Sol round 2).
 247: $att = [ordered]@{
 248:     schema              = 2
 249:     repo                = (Split-Path $toplevel -Leaf)
 250:     mode                = $Mode
 251:     base_sha            = $baseFull
 252:     head_sha            = $headFull
 253:     verdict             = $Verdict
 254:     verification_status = $VerificationStatus
 255:     rounds              = $Rounds
 256:     participants        = $Participants
 257:     route_note          = $RouteNote
 258:     checkpoint_binding  = "none"
 259:     stamp               = (Get-Date -Format "yyyy-MM-ddTHH:mm:ss")
 260: }
 261: if ($CheckpointFile) {
 262:     if (-not (Test-Path $CheckpointFile)) {
 263:         Write-Output "ERROR: checkpoint file not found: $CheckpointFile"
 264:         exit 2
 265:     }
 266:     $cpFull = (Resolve-Path $CheckpointFile).Path
 267:     $cpDir = Join-Path (Join-Path $commonDir "parallax") "application-checkpoints"
 268:     $cpDirFull = if (Test-Path $cpDir) { (Resolve-Path $cpDir).Path } else { $null }
 269:     if ((-not $cpDirFull) -or ((Split-Path $cpFull -Parent) -ne $cpDirFull)) {
 270:         Write-Output "ERROR: checkpoint must live under $cpDir - the verifier re-hashes it there"
 271:         exit 2
 272:     }
 273:     $sha = [System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::Create()
 274:     $bytes = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllBytes($cpFull)
 275:     $cpHash = ([System.BitConverter]::ToString($sha.ComputeHash($bytes)) -replace '-', '').ToLower()
 276:     $changed = @(& git -C $RepoRoot diff --name-only ($baseFull + ".." + $headFull) 2>$null | Where-Object { $_ })
 277:     if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
 278:         Write-Output "ERROR: could not compute the changed-path set for $baseFull..$headFull"
 279:         exit 2
 280:     }
 281:     $att["checkpoint_binding"] = "bound"
 282:     $att["checkpoint_file"] = (Split-Path $CheckpointFile -Leaf)
 283:     $att["checkpoint_hash"] = $cpHash
 284:     $att["changed_paths"] = $changed
 285: }
 286: $outFile = Join-Path $attDir ($headFull + ".json")
 287: try {
 288:     $att | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 3 | Set-Content -Path $outFile -Encoding ASCII -ErrorAction Stop
 289: } catch {
 290:     Write-Output ("ERROR: the attestation could not be written to " + $outFile + ": " + $_.Exception.Message + " - nothing was reaped")
 291:     exit 2
 292: }
 293: if (-not [System.IO.File]::Exists($outFile)) {
 294:     Write-Output ("ERROR: the attestation is not on disk after the write (" + $outFile + ") - nothing was reaped")
 295:     exit 2
 296: }
 297: Write-Output "attestation written: $outFile ($Verdict, $baseFull..$headFull)"
 298: # THE REAP, after the record and in this order: mirror, its advisory
 299: # sidecar, then the bridge. A failure stops at the first tree that
 300: # could not be removed (exit 3) and leaves the record standing.
 301: if ($reapMirrorFull) {
 302:     $bridgeNote = ""
 303:     if ($reapBridgeFull) { $bridgeNote = "; the bridge was not attempted: " + $reapBridgeFull }
 304:     Invoke-Reap "mirror" $reapMirrorFull $bridgeNote
 305:     # The mirror tool's advisory sibling, `<mirror>.source-manifest`,
 306:     # removed only when it is an ordinary file: a directory or a link
 307:     # there is not the sidecar and is left alone.
 308:     $sidecar = $reapMirrorFull + ".source-manifest"
 309:     $sa = $null
 310:     try {
 311:         $sa = [int][System.IO.File]::GetAttributes($sidecar)
 312:     } catch {
 313:         $sa = $null
 314:     }
 315:     if (($null -ne $sa) -and
 316:         (($sa -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::Directory) -eq 0) -and
 317:         (($sa -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::ReparsePoint) -eq 0)) {
 318:         try {
 319:             [System.IO.File]::SetAttributes($sidecar, [System.IO.FileAttributes]::Normal)
 320:             [System.IO.File]::Delete($sidecar)
 321:         } catch {
 322:             Write-Output ("ERROR: reap failed for " + $sidecar + ": " + $_.Exception.Message +
 323:                 " - the attestation stands; remove the sidecar by hand" + $bridgeNote)
 324:             exit 3
 325:         }
 326:         Write-Output ("reaped sidecar: " + $sidecar)
 327:     }
 328: }
 329: if ($reapBridgeFull) {
 330:     Invoke-Reap "bridge" $reapBridgeFull ""
 331: }
 332: exit 0
   1: # review-tree-removal.ps1 - the one removal path for a review tree, and
   2: # the directory-link guard both callers run before it.
   3: #
   4: # DOT-SOURCED, never run:
   5: #   . (Join-Path $PSScriptRoot "review-tree-removal.ps1")
   6: # from tools/new-review-mirror.ps1 (the -Force rebuild, backlog item 98)
   7: # and tools/write-attestation.ps1 (the reap after a terminal verdict,
   8: # backlog item 101). It defines functions and executes nothing else, so
   9: # dot-sourcing it has no effect until a caller calls one.
  10: #
  11: # Windows PowerShell 5.1 compatible, ASCII ONLY.
  12: 
  13: function Test-PathOrAncestorIsLink($path) {
  14:     # The path itself, then each existing ancestor up to the drive root:
  15:     # is any of them a reparse point? Attributes are read directly
  16:     # rather than after a Test-Path, because a DANGLING junction is a
  17:     # reparse point Test-Path may report as absent; a missing entry is
  18:     # the one condition that skips a level, and it is recognised by the
  19:     # exception type, never by a false from a helper. Returns the
  20:     # offending path or $null.
  21:     $probe = ([string]$path).TrimEnd("\", "/")
  22:     while ($probe -and -not [string]::IsNullOrEmpty([System.IO.Path]::GetFileName($probe))) {
  23:         $pa = 0
  24:         $missing = $false
  25:         try {
  26:             $pa = [int][System.IO.File]::GetAttributes($probe)
  27:         } catch [System.IO.FileNotFoundException] {
  28:             $missing = $true
  29:         } catch [System.IO.DirectoryNotFoundException] {
  30:             $missing = $true
  31:         } catch {
  32:             Write-Output ("ERROR: " + $probe + " could not be read while" +
  33:                 " checking for a directory link: " + $_.Exception.Message)
  34:             exit 2
  35:         }
  36:         if (-not $missing -and (($pa -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::ReparsePoint) -ne 0)) {
  37:             return $probe
  38:         }
  39:         $probe = [System.IO.Path]::GetDirectoryName($probe)
  40:     }
  41:     return $null
  42: }
  43: 
  44: function Remove-ReviewTreeEntries($dir, $depth) {
  45:     # Empties $dir in post-order. Returns $null when every entry beneath
  46:     # it is gone, else the reason, which names the entry that stopped it.
  47:     #
  48:     # NEVER THROUGH A LINK. A directory link is removed with the
  49:     # non-recursive Directory.Delete and a file link with File.Delete;
  50:     # both remove the link and never its target. Measured 2026-09-13 on
  51:     # Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7, intact and dangling. An
  52:     # ordinary directory is emptied by this walk and then removed with
  53:     # the same non-recursive Directory.Delete, which throws when
  54:     # anything survived, so a partial removal cannot read as a whole
  55:     # one. An ordinary file has its read-only bit cleared first, because
  56:     # git marks its object files read-only and File.Delete refuses them
  57:     # as they are (measured the same day).
  58:     if ($depth -gt 128) {
  59:         return ($dir + " sits deeper than 128 levels; the walk stops" +
  60:             " rather than assume the tree ends")
  61:     }
  62:     $entries = $null
  63:     try {
  64:         $entries = [System.IO.Directory]::GetFileSystemEntries($dir)
  65:     } catch {
  66:         return ($dir + " could not be listed: " + $_.Exception.Message)
  67:     }
  68:     foreach ($entry in $entries) {
  69:         $ea = 0
  70:         try {
  71:             $ea = [int][System.IO.File]::GetAttributes($entry)
  72:         } catch {
  73:             return ($entry + " could not be examined: " + $_.Exception.Message)
  74:         }
  75:         $isDir = (($ea -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::Directory) -ne 0)
  76:         $isLink = (($ea -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::ReparsePoint) -ne 0)
  77:         try {
  78:             if ($isLink) {
  79:                 if ($isDir) {
  80:                     [System.IO.Directory]::Delete($entry)
  81:                 } else {
  82:                     [System.IO.File]::Delete($entry)
  83:                 }
  84:             } elseif ($isDir) {
  85:                 $inner = Remove-ReviewTreeEntries $entry ($depth + 1)
  86:                 if ($null -ne $inner) { return $inner }
  87:                 # robocopy /DCOPY:DA carries a read-only directory into the mirror; Directory.Delete refuses it as it is (measured 2026-09-13, both hosts).
  88:                 if (($ea -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::ReadOnly) -ne 0) {
  89:                     [System.IO.File]::SetAttributes($entry, [System.IO.FileAttributes]::Directory)
  90:                 }
  91:                 [System.IO.Directory]::Delete($entry)
  92:             } else {
  93:                 if (($ea -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::ReadOnly) -ne 0) {
  94:                     [System.IO.File]::SetAttributes($entry, [System.IO.FileAttributes]::Normal)
  95:                 }
  96:                 [System.IO.File]::Delete($entry)
  97:             }
  98:         } catch {
  99:             return ($entry + " could not be removed: " + $_.Exception.Message)
 100:         }
 101:     }
 102:     return $null
 103: }
 104: 
 105: function Remove-ReviewTree($root) {
 106:     # Removes the tree at $root. Returns @{ Ok = $true }, or
 107:     # @{ Ok = $false; Reason = <text> } the moment any step fails, with
 108:     # the entry that failed named in the reason. It never exits the
 109:     # caller and never writes output, so the caller decides what a
 110:     # failure costs: the mirror tool refuses to build, the emitter
 111:     # reports a written attestation with an unreaped tree.
 112:     #
 113:     # The root's OWN guards are here; the caller runs the ancestor-link
 114:     # guard (Test-PathOrAncestorIsLink) and its overlap comparisons
 115:     # before ever reaching this, because those are refusals that must
 116:     # cost nothing, while this runs after the emitter has written.
 117:     $full = $null
 118:     try {
 119:         $full = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath([string]$root).TrimEnd("\")
 120:     } catch {
 121:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = ("the path could not be resolved (" +
 122:             $root + "): " + $_.Exception.Message) }
 123:     }
 124:     $pathRoot = $null
 125:     try {
 126:         $pathRoot = [System.IO.Path]::GetPathRoot($full)
 127:     } catch {
 128:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = ("the path has no root (" + $full +
 129:             "): " + $_.Exception.Message) }
 130:     }
 131:     if ((-not $pathRoot) -or ($full.Length -le ([string]$pathRoot).TrimEnd("\").Length)) {
 132:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = ($full + " is a filesystem root and" +
 133:             " is never removed") }
 134:     }
 135:     $attr = 0
 136:     try {
 137:         $attr = [int][System.IO.File]::GetAttributes($full)
 138:     } catch [System.IO.FileNotFoundException] {
 139:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = ($full + " does not exist") }
 140:     } catch [System.IO.DirectoryNotFoundException] {
 141:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = ($full + " does not exist") }
 142:     } catch {
 143:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = ($full + " could not be examined: " +
 144:             $_.Exception.Message) }
 145:     }
 146:     if (($attr -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::ReparsePoint) -ne 0) {
 147:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = ($full + " is a link, and a tree is" +
 148:             " never removed through a link") }
 149:     }
 150:     if (($attr -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::Directory) -eq 0) {
 151:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = ($full + " is a file, not a tree") }
 152:     }
 153:     $failure = Remove-ReviewTreeEntries $full 0
 154:     if ($null -ne $failure) {
 155:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = $failure }
 156:     }
 157:     try {
 158:         # robocopy /DCOPY:DA carries a read-only directory into the mirror; Directory.Delete refuses it as it is (measured 2026-09-13, both hosts).
 159:         if (($attr -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::ReadOnly) -ne 0) {
 160:             [System.IO.File]::SetAttributes($full, [System.IO.FileAttributes]::Directory)
 161:         }
 162:         [System.IO.Directory]::Delete($full)
 163:     } catch {
 164:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = ($full + " could not be removed: " +
 165:             $_.Exception.Message) }
 166:     }
 167:     # THE POSTCONDITION, read back rather than inferred from the absence
 168:     # of an exception. Item 98 is a removal that checked nothing after.
 169:     try {
 170:         [void][System.IO.File]::GetAttributes($full)
 171:     } catch [System.IO.FileNotFoundException] {
 172:         return @{ Ok = $true }
 173:     } catch [System.IO.DirectoryNotFoundException] {
 174:         return @{ Ok = $true }
 175:     } catch {
 176:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = ($full + " could not be re-examined" +
 177:             " after removal: " + $_.Exception.Message) }
 178:     }
 179:     return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = ($full + " still exists after removal") }
 180: }

codex
The main removal function checks that the root is gone, and the reap identity reads are pinned to each tree’s `.git`. I found two gaps to examine in the emitter tail: the record check only tests file existence, and sidecar deletion reports success without re-examining the path.

docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-13-mirror-reaper-design.md
   1: # Mirror reaper design
   2: 
   3: Written 2026-09-13 from the KitnEssentials handoff
   4: `dev/docs/handoffs/parallax-mirror-reaper-handoff.md` (outside this
   5: repo). Backlog item 101 holds the measurement; item 98 is the paired
   6: defect this design closes alongside.
   7: 
   8: ## The problem
   9: 
  10: Nothing in the plugin deletes a review mirror. On 2026-09-13 the drive
  11: root held 78 `kv-*` / `kvs-*` directories totalling 13.4 GB, every one
  12: created between 2026-09-09 and 2026-09-13 by multi-model-verify rounds:
  13: about 3 GB per active review day. `kv-<tag>` is the review mirror the
  14: plugin builds; `kvs-<tag>` is the drive-root clone bridge a session
  15: builds first when the reviewed tree is a linked worktree, so the mirror
  16: never copies a `.git` pointer file. The count also grew because the
  17: mirror tool's existing-path refusal suggests `-Force`, and a session
  18: that did not want to rebuild in place built `kv-<tag>-2` beside it.
  19: 
  20: ## The decision on the clone bridge
  21: 
  22: The plugin never created `kvs-*` and cannot recognise one by shape. Of
  23: the three routes the handoff named:
  24: 
  25: - The mirror tool learning a `-SourceBridge` parameter would make it
  26:   build clones, which its own header forbids for good reason (a clone
  27:   carries tracked files only) and would move a KitnEssentials-only
  28:   workaround into a project-agnostic tool. Rejected.
  29: - A prose rule in the KitnEssentials memory alone is what exists today
  30:   for every other artifact, and the measurement above is what a prose
  31:   rule produces. Rejected as the only mechanism.
  32: - **Chosen:** the attestation emitter accepts the bridge as an EXPLICIT
  33:   argument, `-ReapBridge <path>`, beside `-ReapMirror <path>`. The
  34:   session that built the bridge names it; the plugin removes what it is
  35:   told under the same identity guard it applies to the mirror. The
  36:   session-side rule "pass the bridge to the emitter" belongs in the
  37:   KitnEssentials memory that describes the bridge, and this design
  38:   records that as the consumer's follow-up rather than doing it here.
  39: 
  40: ## The reap point is the attestation
  41: 
  42: A round's `resume` re-verifies the mirror's identity, so a mirror
  43: deleted mid-debate turns a resumable round into a transport failure.
  44: The one terminal event the plugin already records mechanically is the
  45: attestation, so `tools/write-attestation.ps1` is the reaper: it
  46: validates every reap path BEFORE writing the record, writes the record,
  47: then removes the trees. No time-based sweep exists anywhere, and the
  48: doctor only reports.
  49: 
  50: Stated limit: a PLAN-mode debate builds the same mirror through preflight
  51: step 3 and ends with a frozen plan, not an attestation, so its mirror has
  52: no mechanical reap point. Its removal is the hand route the doctor names
  53: until a plan-mode terminal event is recorded mechanically; backlog item
  54: 102 carries that residual beside the same-head one.
  55: 
  56: ## The identity guard
  57: 
  58: A reap path is accepted only when ALL of these hold, checked before the
  59: attestation is written so a refused argument costs nothing:
  60: 
  61: 1. It is rooted, resolves, and is not a filesystem root.
  62: 2. It exists and is a directory.
  63: 3. Neither it nor any existing ancestor is a reparse point (the mirror
  64:    tool's own `Test-PathOrAncestorIsLink`, moved into a shared file and
  65:    dot-sourced by both tools rather than copied).
  66: 4. It is not equal to, inside, or containing the reviewed repository's
  67:    top level or its git common dir (the mirror tool's own overlap
  68:    comparisons, same spelling rule).
  69: 5. Its `.git` entry is a DIRECTORY. A `.git` FILE is a linked worktree,
  70:    which is never a mirror or a bridge, and deleting one corrupts the
  71:    primary's worktree list.
  72: 6. Its `HEAD` resolves to the attested head. For the mirror only, a
  73:    HEAD whose author email is `parallax@local` and whose single parent
  74:    is the attested head is also accepted, because that is the
  75:    remediation commit the mirror tool makes over a tracked back-channel.
  76:    A bridge has no such commit and must match exactly.
  77: 
  78: Rule 6 is what binds the reap to the debate that just ended: another
  79: chat's mirror at another head is refused by name, and a bridge the
  80: session forgot to fetch after a fix commit is refused rather than
  81: deleted under a stale head.
  82: 
  83: ## The removal
  84: 
  85: One function, `Remove-ReviewTree`, in the shared file
  86: `tools/review-tree-removal.ps1`, used by the emitter's reap and by the
  87: mirror tool's `-Force` rebuild (item 98). It walks the tree itself in
  88: post-order and never recurses through a reparse point: a directory link
  89: is removed with `[System.IO.Directory]::Delete(link)` and a file link
  90: with `[System.IO.File]::Delete(link)`, both of which remove the link and
  91: never its target (measured 2026-09-13 on both hosts, intact and
  92: dangling); an ordinary file has its read-only bit cleared first,
  93: because git objects carry it; an ordinary directory is emptied then
  94: removed with the non-recursive `Directory.Delete`, which throws when
  95: anything survived. Every exception terminates the removal with the
  96: entry's path in the reason. After the walk the root is re-examined and
  97: must be gone. The function returns `@{ Ok = $true }` or
  98: `@{ Ok = $false; Reason = <text> }`; it never exits the caller.
  99: 
 100: The mirror's `<mirror>.source-manifest` sibling, when it exists as an
 101: ordinary file, is removed after the mirror.
 102: 
 103: ## Exit codes and messages
 104: 
 105: - Emitter: `0` written (and reaped when asked); `2` argument or repo
 106:   error, which now includes every reap-path refusal, made before the
 107:   record is written; `3` the attestation was written and a reap failed,
 108:   named on stdout as `ERROR: reap failed for <path>: <reason>`, with
 109:   the record left standing.
 110: - Mirror tool: an existing `-MirrorPath` without `-Force` is refused
 111:   with a message that names `write-attestation.ps1 -ReapMirror` as the
 112:   route for a finished debate and `-Force` as the in-place rebuild for
 113:   a live one. A `-Force` removal that fails terminates with
 114:   `ERROR: the existing mirror could not be removed: <reason>` and exit
 115:   `2` before anything is created or copied.
 116: 
 117: ## Doctor
 118: 
 119: `/parallax:doctor` gains check 10, an inventory of directories named
 120: `kv*` directly under the system drive root and directly under
 121: `$env:TEMP`: count, total size, oldest `LastWriteTime`. Under 5 GB and
 122: under 3 days old it is a NOTE (OK); at or over either threshold it is
 123: STALE and names the reap route. It never deletes.
 124: 
 125: ## Out of scope
 126: 
 127: Deleting the ten directories that exist today: two other live chats own
 128: them. The KitnEssentials memory edit that tells the bridge builder to
 129: pass `-ReapBridge`. Item 99 (short-name aliases).
skills/multi-model-verify/references/preflight-mirror.md
   1: # Preflight: building the review mirror
   2: 
   3: Construction detail for SKILL.md's preflight item 3, "build the review
   4: mirror" step. This is read only when the enumeration there actually found
   5: an `AGENTS.md`, `.agents/*`, or `.kimi-code/*` entry and
   6: SKILL.md's back-channel-auto-mirror requires the mirror — the operational
   7: imperative and the do-not-ask rule stay in SKILL.md itself; this is the
   8: how, not the whether.
   9: 
  10: Run
  11: `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1 -RepoRoot <repo> -MirrorPath <scratch>`.
  12: Build at a SHORT `<scratch>` directly under the temp directory, such
  13: as a `kv-<tag>` folder, never inside the session scratchpad: the
  14: mirror re-roots every path, and the tool refuses before creating
  15: anything when the budget is blown. That location is the
  16: `Canonical review mirror root` row of references/model-prompting-notes.md's
  17: round-artifact-roots declaration, fixed there because the tool refuses a
  18: mirror inside the reviewed repository.
  19: It builds the **review mirror** (references/backup-lane.md owns its
  20: construction, its baseline, and its identity fields — a file copy
  21: preserving `.git`, NOT a clone), deletes the offending entries THERE,
  22: commits when any were tracked, re-runs the enumeration from SKILL.md's
  23: preflight item 3 inside the mirror, captures the baseline and the
  24: content manifest, runs the client probe from that same preflight step
  25: with the mirror as the working directory, and prints the record block;
  26: empty enumeration output is the evidence, and the mirror's identity
  27: fields go in the debate record. The mirror is then the reviewed tree for
  28: every lane in that debate — dispatch codex with the mirror as cwd, and
  29: keep citations resolvable in the real repo. Whether the removal needs a
  30: commit branches on tracked-ness, and the difference misreads as a
  31: failure; references/backup-lane.md states that branch and the hook
  32: behaviour that comes with it.
  33: 
  34: Files above the repo's git root are NOT ingested (same probe), and
  35: `~/.codex/AGENTS.md` is the user's own
  36: global instruction file — note it in the debate record if it exists,
  37: but it is not a stop.
  38: 
  39: ## The quiet period
  40: 
  41: <!-- contract:start id=mirror-quiet-period -->
  42: NOTHING MAY WRITE INSIDE THE REVIEWED REPOSITORY from the moment the
  43: mirror is built until the wrapper exits. The identity digest covers the
  44: fields of `git status --porcelain --ignored` PLUS the content of the
  45: paths that listing names, ignored ones included, with a directory
  46: expanded to its files and a deletion-only entry contributing no bytes.
  47: So a test-cache write, a plan-ledger append, a drift report or one new
  48: untracked file is enough. The same recorded digest is compared against
  49: the live source three times: at preparation, before the client runs,
  50: and after it finishes. The last of those spans the whole round, so this
  51: is a quiet period and not an ordering rule. Only that last one costs a
  52: reviewer round; the two before it refuse before the client is invoked
  53: and spend no quota. State the limits with the rule: the comparison
  54: samples endpoints, so a change made and reverted inside the round is
  55: not detected, and a tracked file git reports CLEAN is covered by
  56: neither fingerprint. Queue every edit until the wrapper exits, however
  57: small and however unrelated it looks.
  58: <!-- contract:end -->
  59: 
  60: ## Timing
  61: 
  62: BUILD THE MIRROR LAST. Every act that writes inside the reviewed
  63: repository finishes first: the gates, the plan ledger, the scratch notes,
  64: the formatter. From the build until the round's wrapper exits, the
  65: repository is quiet, and preflight-mirror.md's mirror-quiet-period states
  66: why. The identity digest covers the content of ignored paths, so a
  67: pytest cache directory or a ledger append is enough to refuse the
  68: dispatch.
  69: 
  70: A build that has gone stale is not repaired and cannot be re-blessed:
  71: there is deliberately no re-mint or reseal mode. READ THE EXPLANATION
  72: FIRST, then build again. Rebuilding replaces the evidence of what
  73: changed, so a rebuild before reading turns a diagnosable refusal into an
  74: unexplained one. The rebuild itself is cheap next to a spent round,
  75: measured at about 92 seconds on a repo carrying a linked reference
  76: checkout.
  77: 
  78: When a refusal names `the source status changed since construction`, the
  79: lines beneath it name the paths whose content changed and the paths that
  80: entered or left manifest coverage, read from the `source_manifest` file
  81: the record block points at. That explanation is advisory: it can be
  82: missing, incomplete or wrong, and the refusal stands either way.
  83: Manifest coverage is not file existence, so a path listed as leaving
  84: coverage has not necessarily been deleted.
  85: 
  86: The two refusals raised by the round wrapper itself print no explanation
  87: to the console. The wrapper redirects both identity checks into
  88: `mirror.verify` inside its dispatch directory and then throws a short
  89: message, so that file is where the detail is.
  90: 
  91: ## End of life
  92: 
  93: The attestation is the reap point. A round's `resume` re-verifies the
  94: mirror's identity, so a mirror deleted mid-debate turns a resumable
  95: round into a transport failure; the one terminal event the plugin
  96: records mechanically is the attestation, so `tools/write-attestation.ps1`
  97: removes the mirror when it is passed as `-ReapMirror <path>`, and the
  98: clone bridge a linked worktree needed when it is passed as
  99: `-ReapBridge <path>`. The reap is bound to that event and never an age:
 100: no sweep exists, and the doctor only reports.
 101: 
 102: The emitter validates both paths BEFORE it writes the record, so a wrong
 103: argument is refused at exit 2 with nothing written, and removes them
 104: AFTER, so a removal that fails leaves the verdict standing and exits 3
 105: naming the entry that stopped it. A path is accepted only when it
 106: exists as a directory not reached through a link, does not overlap the
 107: reviewed repository or its git common dir, holds a `.git` DIRECTORY
 108: (a `.git` FILE marks a linked worktree, never a mirror or a bridge), and
 109: its HEAD is the attested head. The mirror may instead sit exactly one
 110: `parallax@local` remediation commit above that head, because that is the
 111: commit construction makes over a tracked back-channel; the bridge must
 112: match exactly, so a bridge left unfetched after a fix commit is refused
 113: rather than deleted under a stale head. Measured 2026-09-13: 78 mirror
 114: and bridge directories, 13.4 GB, in four review days, with nothing but
 115: memory saying which of them a live chat could still resume. Another chat's mirror at another head is refused by name; the guard cannot tell two trees at the SAME head apart, so the session names only the trees it built, and the residual is backlog item 102.
 116: 
 117: The removal never recurses through a link: `tools/review-tree-removal.ps1`
 118: walks the tree itself, removes each link as a link, clears the read-only
 119: bit git puts on its objects, and re-examines the root afterwards. The
 120: mirror tool's `-Force` rebuild uses the same function, which is what
 121: closed backlog item 98.
 122: 
 123: An existing `-MirrorPath` without `-Force` is refused with the reap
 124: route named. Build `kv-<tag>-2` beside a finished debate's mirror and
 125: the count grows by one for every debate; reap the finished one instead,
 126: and rebuild in place with `-Force` only for a debate that is still
 127: running, because a resumed round needs the mirror at the path its
 128: identity was recorded at.
 129: 
 130: The bridge is the session's. The plugin never created it and cannot
 131: recognise one by shape, so the session that built it names it; the rule
 132: "pass the bridge to the emitter" belongs beside the rule that builds it.
 133: 
 134: Two limits, stated. A plan-mode debate ends with a frozen plan and no
 135: attestation, so its mirror has no mechanical reap point and keeps the
 136: hand route until one exists (backlog item 102). And an ESCALATE the user
 137: may still extend is not yet terminal: emit the attestation, and with it
 138: the reap, only once the user has declined to extend, because a reaped
 139: mirror turns the extension's `resume` into a transport failure.
 280:    $seen = ([System.BitConverter]::ToString(([System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::Create()).ComputeHash($bytes)) -replace '-', '').ToLower()
 281:    if ($seen -cne "<override-sha256>") { throw "the override file changed after the probe verified it" }
 282:    $override = (New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false, $true)).GetString($bytes)
 283:    $brief | codex exec --sandbox read-only --disable plugins --disable apps --disable memories -c mcp_servers.node_repl.enabled=false -c $override -m <canonical-model-id> -c model_reasoning_effort=<canonical-effort> --output-last-message $PSScriptRoot/reply resume <SESSION_ID> - > $PSScriptRoot/transcript 2>&1
 284:    $code = $LASTEXITCODE
 285:    } catch { $code = 1 } finally { $OutputEncoding = $priorOutputEncoding }
 286:    [System.IO.File]::WriteAllText("$PSScriptRoot/exit", "$code")
 287:    exit $code
 288:    ```
 289: 
 290:    Run `-Prepare` the same way as round 1, with `-DispatchHost` and
 291:    `-WorkdirEvidence` set the same way. A resumed round needs the
 292:    mirror at the path its identity was recorded at: if the tree had to be
 293:    rebuilt mid-debate, rebuild it at the SAME path
 294:    with `-Force` and re-record its identity fields, because a mirror at a new
 295:    path makes the binder refuse the resumed slice on `cwd` and the round is
 296:    lost. If it cannot be rebuilt there, dispatch FRESH rather than resume.
 297: 
 298:    ```powershell
 299:    & (Get-Process -Id $PID).Path -NoProfile -File ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/tools/dispatch-round.ps1 -Prepare -DispatchDir <dispatch-dir> -WrapperBody <wrapper-file> -ReceiptPath <receipt-file> -Round <label> -WorkingDirectory <mirror-path> -RepoRoot <repo-root> -SourceHead <source-head> -MirrorHead <mirror-head> -SourceStatusSha256 <source-status-sha256> -MirrorStateSha256 <mirror-state-sha256> -ExpectedMirrorPath <mirror-path> -DispatchHost <dispatch-host> -PriorStateFile <prior-state-file> -WorkdirEvidence <mirror-path> -Json
 300:    ```
 301: 
 302:    `-Prepare` prints `command` and `taskName`: dispatch it as a harness background command, running `command` exactly as printed, under the `taskName` the tool printed, and STOP — but never END THE TURN with the round unfinished (references/model-prompting-notes.md's round-dispatch-operation).
 303: 
 304:    On the completion notification for that exact task, read the harness output file: the exit code of that exact task is the result, never re-read the dispatch directory for a verdict — **0 means `reply-present` and nothing else; 2 is a parameter-binding failure or an internal execution error; 1 is every other state, named on the wrapper's last stdout line.** The states -Classify computes are references/model-prompting-notes.md's round-dispatch-states.
 305: 
 306:    Bind the reply the same way as round 1, with `-Resume` in place of
 307:    `-Fresh`, and pass `-SealedPriorStateSha256` with THIS round's
 308:    `priorStateSha256`, captured immediately before this dispatch.
 309: 
 310:    The preamble repeats in full every round. Rounds are separate shell
 311:    invocations, so a `$override` set in round 1 does not exist in round 3,
 312:    and one verification does not cover a file that can change between
 313:    rounds.
 314: 
 315:    Each resume's header must echo the resumed `session id:` and the same
 316:    effective route — the round-1 check repeated, `sandbox:` included.
 317:    Sandbox mode has NO continuity across resumes, so the flag is re-pinned
 318:    on every one; references/model-prompting-notes.md carries the probe that
 319:    measured it and what a dropped flag does.
 320: 
 321: 4. Iterate per debate-protocol.md until convergence or the round cap, then
 322:    escalate any unresolved points to the user with both positions stated.
 323: 5. Freeze the converged plan per references/frozen-plan-format.md at the
 324:    frozen-plan path preflight step 4 printed.
 325: 
 326: ## Mode diff
 327: 
 328: Same transport and protocol. First read the frozen plan's debate record and
 329: its **Verification status** field.
 330: 
 331: Required before round 1: the agents/fable-reviewer.md whole-branch review runs on the same range, its raw reply is retained as a range-bound artifact, and the round-1 brief cites that artifact with the session's per-finding adjudications.
 332: The session adjudicates each review finding with evidence — accept,
 333: refute, or ESCALATE into the debate — before any reviewer lane sees
 334: them; the review is the debate's required input, never its verdict,
 335: and the debate record names the artifact path.
 336: 
 337: Then the brief carries the frozen plan
 338: path, the base/head SHAs superpowers code review used, and the
 339: `git diff base..head` output. Both sides check **spec fidelity** (drift from
 340: the frozen plan — the implementer makes zero judgment calls, so any drift is
 341: a finding, with one carve-out: envelope-designated escalation-lane DECISIONS
 342: are adjudicated against their frozen envelope per
 343: references/frozen-plan-format.md, and only envelope overruns are drift) and,
 344: for port work, **port fidelity** (drift from the reference
 345: source), ending PASS / FIX / ESCALATE.
 346: 
 347: Backup lane: same protocol, transport and per-round evidence per references/backup-lane.md.
 348: 
 349: Panels: any reviewer-lane combination per references/panels.md.
 350: 
 351: A FIX this session applies itself goes through the **application
 352: checkpoint** (references/application-checkpoint.md) before the first edit.
 353: The fixed range is then re-reviewed, and only the post-re-review terminal
 354: PASS is attested — with the emitter's `-CheckpointFile` binding the
 355: checkpoint; a FIX verdict whose fixes are still unapplied is never the
 356: attested verdict.
 357: 
 358: **Degraded-plan poisoning rule:** a plan whose Verification status is
 359: DEGRADED cannot produce an ordinary diff PASS. Mode diff must first reopen
 360: and cross-verify the plan's claims and approved deviations (the
 361: retrospective pass the plan never got), and only then verify the
 362: implementation against it — confirming a diff matches an unsound plan
 363: verifies nothing. If cross-vendor verification is STILL unavailable, the
 364: only terminal state is `ESCALATE — CROSS-VENDOR GATE UNSATISFIED`: merging
 365: then requires the user's explicitly recorded risk acceptance, never a PASS.
 366: 
 367: ## Finish line
 368: 
 369: Before it: the session's final-adjudication step (debate-protocol.md) —
 370: verify the last round's findings against the repo and issue the terminal
 371: verdict; a reviewer's PASS/FIX is never terminal by itself.
 372: 
 373: A PASS is terminal only for the exact head it was issued on. If you apply
 374: anything the reviewer raised — including observations it labelled
 375: non-blocking — the head moves and the verdict no longer covers it. Either
 376: leave them for a follow-up branch, or run one confirming round.
 377: 
 378: **Mode diff only — record the verdict mechanically.** After the terminal
 379: verdict, run the attestation emitter from this plugin's checkout:
 380: 
 381: ```powershell
 382: powershell -NoProfile -File <plugin-root>/tools/write-attestation.ps1 -RepoRoot <reviewed-repo> -BaseSha <base> -HeadSha <head> -Verdict <PASS|FIX|ESCALATE> -VerificationStatus <FULL|DEGRADED> -RouteNote "<effective route confirmed | the transport-failure class>" -Rounds <n> -Participants "<session-model> (session) / <reviewer-model> (reviewer)" [-CheckpointFile <application-checkpoint-artifact>] [-ReapMirror <mirror>] [-ReapBridge <bridge>]
 383: ```
 384: 
 385: When an application checkpoint governed fix application, pass it via
 386: `-CheckpointFile`; references/application-checkpoint.md states what that
 387: binds and which head it is bound to.
 388: 
 389: Pass the mirror and the clone bridge as `-ReapMirror`/`-ReapBridge`;
 390: the End of life section of references/preflight-mirror.md states the guard.
 391: 
 392: It writes the attestation row's path, which preflight step 4 printed —
 393: untracked by design, so recording the verdict cannot move HEAD out from
 394: under its own SHA. The pre-push lane (`tools/verify-attestation.ps1`)
 395: later warns when a `main` push has no matching attestation for the pushed
 396: head (fast-forward: pushed sha == attested head; merge commit: parent1 ==
 397: attested base, parent2 == attested head — a squash changes the sha and
 398: correctly forces re-review).
 399: 
 400: End with one status line: participating models, rounds used, converged vs
 401: escalated points, the verification status — `FULL`, or
 402: `DEGRADED (<class>, authorized by user at round N)` per fallbacks.md — and
 403: the route note: `effective route confirmed` when every round's header
 404: matched the canonical declarations, else the transport-failure class that
 405: fallbacks.md handled. The route-note grammar is lane-agnostic: for a backup-lane debate,
 406: `effective route confirmed` means every round satisfied
 407: references/backup-lane.md's per-round evidence rules against the backup
 408: declarations; the evidence class is recorded in the debate record prose. The session emits this line itself; never delegate it
 409: to a subagent.
 410: 
 411: ## Common mistakes
 412: 
 413: - Accepting the reviewer's claims about reference code without the cited lines —
 414:   strike the claim per protocol; do not argue against it.
 415: - Running mode `diff` against different SHAs than the code review used.

 314:     repo, base, head = make_repo(tmp_path)
 315:     keep = None
 316:     if shape == "missing":
 317:         path = tmp_path / "absent"
 318:     elif shape == "file":

 323:         make_mirror(repo / ".git", path / ".git")

 439: def test_a_failed_record_write_reaps_nothing(tmp_path):

 476: @pytest.mark.parametrize("shape", ["same", "nested"])
 477: def test_overlapping_mirror_and_bridge_are_refused(tmp_path, shape):
 478:     repo, base, head = make_repo(tmp_path)
 479:     if shape == "same":
 480:         one = make_bridge(repo, tmp_path / "kvs-t")
 481:         mirror = one
 482:         bridge = one
 483:     else:
 484:         mirror = make_mirror(repo, tmp_path / "kv-t")
 485:         bridge = make_bridge(repo, mirror / "kvs-inner")
 486:     proc = attest(repo, base, head, mirror=mirror, bridge=bridge)
 487:     assert proc.returncode == 2, shape + ": " + proc.stdout + proc.stderr

   2: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-13-mirror-reaper-design.md).
   3: 
   4: Four groups. REMOVAL: tools/review-tree-removal.ps1's Remove-ReviewTree,
   5: driven through a harness that dot-sources it, removes a tree with
   6: read-only files and a junction whose target survives, and reports a
   7: named failure on a held handle, a missing path and a link. EMITTER:
   8: tools/write-attestation.ps1's reap parameters refuse every wrong tree
   9: before writing the record and remove the right ones after. MIRROR: the
  10: mirror tool's rebuild removal goes through the same function and its
  11: existing-path refusal names the reap route. PROSE: the skill, the
  12: reference and the doctor carry the rule.

  14: WINDOWS ONLY: junctions and the held-handle sharing rule are Windows

  44: def run_ps(script, *args):
  45:     return subprocess.run(
  46:         [POWERSHELL, "-NoProfile", "-NonInteractive", "-ExecutionPolicy",
  47:          "Bypass", "-File", str(script), *args],
  48:         capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=180)

  51: def git(repo, *args):
  52:     return subprocess.run(
  53:         ["git", "-C", str(repo), "-c", "user.name=reap-test",
  54:          "-c", "user.email=t@localhost", *args],
  55:         check=True, capture_output=True, text=True).stdout.strip()

  57: 
  58: def make_repo(tmp_path, name="repo"):
  59:     """A repo with a base commit and a feature commit, returned with both
  60:     SHAs, because an attestation needs a real base..head range."""
  61:     repo = tmp_path / name
  62:     repo.mkdir()
  63:     subprocess.run(["git", "init", "-q", "-b", "main"], cwd=repo,
  64:                    check=True, capture_output=True)
  65:     (repo / "a.txt").write_text("a\n", encoding="utf-8")
  66:     git(repo, "add", "a.txt")
  67:     git(repo, "commit", "-q", "-m", "base")
  68:     base = git(repo, "rev-parse", "HEAD")
  69:     git(repo, "checkout", "-q", "-b", "feat")
  70:     (repo / "b.txt").write_text("b\n", encoding="utf-8")
  71:     git(repo, "add", "b.txt")
  72:     git(repo, "commit", "-q", "-m", "feat")
  73:     head = git(repo, "rev-parse", "HEAD")
  74:     return repo, base, head
  75: 
  76: 
  77: def make_mirror(repo, path):
  78:     """A mirror the way the mirror tool makes one: a FILE COPY that keeps
  79:     .git, so it carries git's read-only object files."""
  80:     shutil.copytree(repo, path)
  81:     return path
  82: 
  83: 
  84: def make_bridge(repo, path, branch="feat"):
  85:     """A clone bridge the way a KitnEssentials session makes one, checked
  86:     out after a --no-checkout clone."""
  87:     subprocess.run(["git", "clone", "-q", "--no-checkout", str(repo), str(path)],
  88:                    check=True, capture_output=True)
  89:     git(path, "checkout", "-q", branch)
  90:     return path
  91: 
  92: 
  93: def junction(link, target):
  94:     subprocess.run(["cmd", "/c", "mklink", "/J", str(link), str(target)],
  95:                    check=True, capture_output=True)
  96: 
  97: 
  98: def read(path):
  99:     assert path.is_file(), f"missing file: {path}"
 100:     return path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")

 108:     '. "{removal}"\n'
 109:     '$r = Remove-ReviewTree $Target\n'

 227: def attest(repo, base, head, mirror=None, bridge=None):
 228:     args = [WRITE, "-RepoRoot", str(repo), "-BaseSha", base, "-HeadSha", head,
 229:             "-Verdict", "PASS", "-VerificationStatus", "FULL",
 230:             "-RouteNote", "effective route confirmed", "-Rounds", "1",
 231:             "-Participants", "session/reviewer"]
 232:     if mirror is not None:
 233:         args += ["-ReapMirror", str(mirror)]
 234:     if bridge is not None:
 235:         args += ["-ReapBridge", str(bridge)]
 236:     return run_ps(*args)

 100: **Filed 2026-09-13 from the whole-branch review of the mirror reaper
 101: (item 101).** The emitter's identity guard refuses a tree that is not
 102: at the attested head, that overlaps the reviewed repository or its
 103: common dir, that is reached through a link, or whose `.git` is a file,
 104: and it pins each git read to the tree's own git dir. What it cannot do
 105: is distinguish this debate's mirror or bridge from any other clone of
 106: the same repository sitting at the same head: a second plain clone
 107: with unpushed branches passes every rule if the session names it. The
 108: prose in references/preflight-mirror.md states the residual instead of
 109: hiding it. A plan-mode debate ends with a frozen plan and no attestation,
 110: so its mirror has no mechanical reap point either; a plan-mode terminal
 111: event recorded mechanically is the third follow-up.
 112: 
 113: **Two follow-ups, one decision each.**
 114: 
 115: 1. The bridge has a marker the mirror does not: a session clones it
 116:    from the reviewed repository, so its `origin` resolves to that
 117:    repository's top level or common dir, while a user's own clone points
 118:    at the remote. Requiring that for `-ReapBridge` closes half the gap
 119:    at one git call. The mirror carries whatever remotes its source had,
 120:    so the same rule does not apply to it without a marker the mirror
 121:    tool would have to write, and the tool writes nothing identifying
 122:    inside the mirror by design (the fingerprint covers every byte).
 123: 2. The location. The canonical review mirror root is `<TEMP>/<short-name>/`,
 124:    but the DT review packets put the deepest file 243 characters below
 125:    the repo root, so the mirror root must be 15 characters or fewer and
 126:    the 36-character temp directory cannot hold one; the sessions build
 127:    at `C:\kv-<tag>` instead, which is why the 2026-09-13 measurement
 128:    found 78 directories at the drive root. A declared short parent such
 129:    as `C:\pxm\<tag>` would satisfy the budget, keep the drive root clear,
 130:    turn the doctor's `kv*` name pattern into a fixed directory, and give
 131:    the reap guard one more cheap rule: a reap path must sit under the
 132:    declared parent. That edits the round-artifact-roots region and its
 133:    pin, `tools/artifact-roots.ps1`, doctor check 10 and the KitnEssentials
 134:    memory that names `C:\kv-<tag>`; the user picks the name.
 135: 
 136: **What closing it means.** The bridge origin rule shipped with a test
 137: that drives a foreign clone at the attested head and sees it refused,
 138: and a decision recorded on the mirror parent, either a new declared
 139: root with the four edits above or a stated reason to keep the drive
 140: root.

 179: **What closing it means.** The reap point is a TERMINAL event, never an
 180: age: `tools/write-attestation.ps1` validates every reap path before it
 181: writes the record, writes it, then removes the trees through one shared
 182: removal function that never recurses through a link, terminates with a
 183: named error on the first failure, and re-examines the root afterwards.
 184: A reap path is accepted only when its `.git` is a directory and its
 185: `HEAD` is the attested head (the mirror may instead sit one
 186: `parallax@local` remediation commit above it), so another chat's mirror
 187: at another head is refused by name. The mirror tool's existing-path

 189: the `kv*` inventory as a note and never deletes. A plan-mode debate has

 191: item 102's. Item 98 closes with it,
